## Job Title
**Prompt Engineer, Agent Prompts & Evals**

**Locations:** San Francisco, CA | New York City, NY

---

## Key Responsibilities

1. Design, test, and optimize system prompts and feature-specific prompts across consumer and API offerings
2. Build and maintain comprehensive evaluation suites ensuring consistency and quality across product launches
3. Partner with product, research, and safety teams on feature quality and safety standards
4. Play a critical role in model releases, identifying issues before user impact
5. Help develop frameworks and tools for prompt and feature testing
6. Mentor engineers on prompt optimization and evaluation building
7. Adapt quickly in fast-paced environments with daily capability advances

---

## Required Qualifications

1. 5+ years of software engineering experience with Python or similar languages
2. Demonstrated LLM and prompt engineering experience through work, research, or projects
3. Strong understanding of evaluation methodologies and metrics for AI systems
4. Excellent written and verbal communication abilities
5. Ability to manage concurrent projects with effective prioritization
6. Experience with version control, CI/CD, and modern development practices

---

## Preferred Qualifications

1. Production experience with Claude or frontier AI models
2. ML, NLP, or related field background
3. A/B testing and experimentation frameworks knowledge
4. AI safety and alignment familiarity
5. ML/AI workflow tooling experience
6. Track record of systematic performance improvement

---

## Desired Qualities & Emphasis

The posting emphasizes candidates who:
- **Get excited about the nuances of how language models behave**
- Bridge research and product effectively
- Thrive with ambiguity
- Demonstrate strong ownership
- Prioritize beneficial AI development
- Collaborate effectively

---

## Compensation
$320,000–$405,000 USD annually

Great — I've read both carefully. Here's my quick synthesis before you share your ideas:

The role alignment is precise. The title is "Prompt Engineer, Agent Prompts & Evals" — the eval suite isn't a box to check, it's the centerpiece of the evaluation. They want to see that you know how to measure things rigorously, iterate on prompts based on evidence, and articulate failure modes. The prompt engineering is table stakes; the eval design thinking is what separates candidates.

What the assignment is really testing: - Can you design a system prompt that guides agentic behavior (when to search, how to form queries, how to reason over retrieved text)? - Can you define meaningful eval dimensions — not just "correct/incorrect" but things like: search decision quality, query formulation quality, multi-hop reasoning, hallucination on missing knowledge, calibration/uncertainty? - Can you show an iteration loop: evals revealed X → I changed the prompt this way → results improved by Y? - Do you have taste? The context section literally says "the best products... solve real problems in non-obvious ways."

Key tension to resolve: The assignment says 1-2 hours target, but there's a lot of surface area. The right call is to go deep on 1-2 interesting eval dimensions rather than broad-but-shallow coverage of 6.

📝 Write spicy-skipping-orbit.md
/Users/dilraj/.claude/plans/spicy-skipping-orbit.md
# Wikipedia QA Agent — Anthropic Take-Home Plan

## Context

Building a Wikipedia-grounded QA agent for the Anthropic Prompt Engineer (Agent Prompts & Evals) take-home assessment. The primary evaluation criteria are: (1) prompt engineering quality and rationale, (2) eval suite rigor and design thinking, (3) authentic iteration loop from evals to prompt improvement. Code quality is secondary.

**Core design stance:** The agent *always* retrieves from Wikipedia before answering (mandatory grounding). This is a deliberate philosophical choice — this system is a grounded research assistant, not a knowledge recall system.

---

## Architecture

```
anthropic_qa_takehome/
  README.md               # Setup instructions, demo commands
  requirements.txt
  .env.example
  demo.py                 # Interactive CLI (main entry point for reviewers)
  run_evals.py            # Eval runner CLI

  src/
    agent.py              # Agentic loop: tool calling + multi-hop + response synthesis
    tools.py              # search_wikipedia() — MediaWiki API wrapper
    prompts.py            # System prompts: PROMPT_V1 and PROMPT_V2 (versioned constants)

  evals/
    dataset.py            # 25 curated test cases with categories + ground truth
    checks.py             # Deterministic assertion checks
    judges/
      grounding.py        # "Is this answer supported by the Wikipedia text retrieved?"
      factuality.py       # "Is this answer factually correct?"
      query_quality.py    # "Were the Wikipedia search queries well-formulated?"
    runner.py             # Orchestrates full eval: deterministic + judges + pairwise

  results/                # Committed JSON results for reviewer inspection
    v1_results.json
    v2_results.json
    comparison.json
```

---

## Component Design

### 1. Wikipedia Tool (`src/tools.py`)

Uses the `wikipedia` Python library (wraps MediaWiki API — no setup required).

```python
def search_wikipedia(query: str) -> dict:
    # Returns: {"title": str, "content": str (first ~600 words), "url": str}
    # On ambiguity: tries disambiguation, returns best match
    # On not found: returns {"error": "No Wikipedia article found for: {query}"}
```

The tool is intentionally simple — the interesting engineering is in the prompt and eval.

### 2. Agent Loop (`src/agent.py`)

Agentic loop using the Anthropic SDK with tool use:
- Max iterations: 5 (prevents runaway loops)
- Returns: `AgentResponse(answer: str, searches: list[SearchResult], model: str, prompt_version: str)`
- Tracks all searches made (for eval purposes)

### 3. System Prompts (`src/prompts.py`)

Two versioned prompts. v1 is built first, v2 is derived from real eval failures.

**PROMPT_V1 — key elements:**
```
Role: Wikipedia-grounded research assistant
Rules:
  1. ALWAYS search Wikipedia before answering factual questions
  2. Only claim what Wikipedia explicitly states
  3. Use multiple searches if the first doesn't cover everything
  4. Cite the Wikipedia article(s) your answer draws from
  5. Express uncertainty when Wikipedia is incomplete or unclear

Query guidance:
  - Search for entity names, not full questions
  - Bad: "What year did Albert Einstein die?"
  - Good: "Albert Einstein"
  - For multi-part questions, plan all needed searches upfront
```

**PROMPT_V2 — designed after v1 eval failures.** Expected failure modes v1 will surface:
- Model doesn't trigger second search for multi-hop questions (fix: explicit multi-hop instruction with example)
- Queries are too verbose / natural-language style (fix: stronger query formatting rules)
- Grounding leaks — model adds training knowledge beyond Wikipedia (fix: stricter grounding rule with "if Wikipedia doesn't say this, don't say it")

---

## Eval Suite Design (`evals/`)

### Test Dataset — 25 cases across 6 categories

| Category | Count | Purpose |
|----------|-------|---------|
| Simple factual | 6 | Baseline — entity lookups with clear ground truth |
| Multi-hop | 5 | Agent must chain 2+ Wikipedia searches |
| Complex/analytical | 4 | Cause-effect, historical explanations |
| Ambiguous queries | 3 | Multiple possible entities (e.g., "Mercury") |
| Edge cases | 4 | Obscure topics, potentially missing Wikipedia coverage |
| Misconceptions | 3 | Common false beliefs — tests faithfulness to Wikipedia |

Example test cases:
- Simple: "When was Marie Curie born?" → deterministic: contains "1867"
- Multi-hop: "What is the birth country of the director of Parasite?" → requires searching Bong Joon-ho after finding Parasite
- Misconception: "Did Einstein fail math in school?" → Wikipedia debunks this
- Ambiguous: "Tell me about Mercury" → agent should clarify or pick context

Each case: `{id, question, category, expected_answer, key_facts: list[str], requires_multihop: bool}`

### Deterministic Checks (`evals/checks.py`)

Fast, assertion-based, no API calls:
- `search_was_used`: at least one Wikipedia search occurred
- `multi_hop_triggered`: for multi-hop questions, ≥2 searches occurred
- `key_facts_present`: expected key facts appear in answer (substring/regex)
- `no_search_refusal`: agent didn't fail to answer due to search error handling

### LLM Judges — Specialized Per Dimension (`evals/judges/`)

All judges use Claude Haiku 4.5 (fast, cheap). Each judge returns `{score: int (1-5), reasoning: str}`.

**Grounding Judge** (`grounding.py`)
- Input: question + retrieved Wikipedia text + agent answer
- Prompt: "Does this answer rely only on what the Wikipedia text says? Does it add facts not in the retrieved text?"
- Score 1 = heavily hallucinated beyond Wikipedia, 5 = fully grounded

**Factuality Judge** (`factuality.py`)
- Input: question + agent answer + ground truth
- Prompt: "Is this answer factually correct? Does it contradict the expected answer?"
- Score 1 = factually wrong, 5 = correct and complete

**Query Quality Judge** (`query_quality.py`)
- Input: original question + list of queries used
- Prompt: "Were these Wikipedia search queries well-formulated? Did they use specific entity names? Would they retrieve the right articles?"
- Score 1 = poor queries (full questions, vague), 5 = precise entity-name queries

### Pairwise Comparison (`evals/runner.py`)

For each question in the dataset, compare v1 and v2 answers:
- Input: question + answer_v1 + answer_v2
- Prompt: "Which answer is better and why? Consider: accuracy, grounding, completeness, clarity."
- Output: `{winner: "v1"|"v2"|"tie", reasoning: str}`

Aggregate pairwise win rate as headline metric for the iteration story.

---

## Model Choices

- **Agent**: `claude-sonnet-4-6` — best reasoning for complex multi-hop questions
- **LLM Judges**: `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` — fast and cheap for bulk eval runs
- Noted in writeup with rationale

---

## Iteration Story (Authentic Loop)

1. Implement PROMPT_V1
2. Run full eval on 25 cases → `results/v1_results.json`
3. Analyze: which categories have lowest scores? What failure patterns appear?
4. Write PROMPT_V2 targeting the top 2-3 specific failures observed
5. Run full eval again → `results/v2_results.json`
6. Run pairwise comparison → `results/comparison.json`
7. Report delta: per-dimension score improvement + pairwise win rate

This is the narrative backbone of the video and written rationale.

---

## CLI Interface

```bash
# Interactive demo (for reviewers)
python demo.py
python demo.py "Who invented the telephone?"

# Run eval suite
python run_evals.py --prompt v1 --output results/v1_results.json
python run_evals.py --prompt v2 --output results/v2_results.json
python run_evals.py --compare results/v1_results.json results/v2_results.json
```

---

## Implementation Order

1. `src/tools.py` — Wikipedia search (10 min)
2. `src/prompts.py` — PROMPT_V1 (20 min)
3. `src/agent.py` — agentic loop with tool use (20 min)
4. `demo.py` — working CLI (10 min)
5. `evals/dataset.py` — 25 test cases (25 min)
6. `evals/checks.py` — deterministic checks (15 min)
7. `evals/judges/` — 3 specialized judges (30 min)
8. `evals/runner.py` — orchestration + pairwise (20 min)
9. Run v1 evals, analyze failures, write PROMPT_V2 (30 min)
10. Run v2 evals, generate comparison (10 min)
11. README + writeup (20 min)

**Estimated total: ~3.5 hours**

---

## Verification

- `python demo.py "Who was the first person to walk on the moon?"` — shows multi-turn search + grounded answer
- `python run_evals.py --prompt v1` — full suite runs without errors, produces JSON
- `python run_evals.py --compare` — pairwise comparison shows v2 wins on targeted categories
- Check `results/v1_results.json` — all 25 cases populated with scores and reasoning

# Wikipedia QA Agent — Design Document

## Context

Anthropic take-home for Prompt Engineer (Agent Prompts & Evals). Primary evaluation criteria: prompt engineering quality, eval suite rigor and design thinking, authentic iteration loop. Code quality is secondary.

**Core design stance:** Agent *always* retrieves from Wikipedia before answering. Mandatory grounding — this is a Wikipedia-grounded assistant, not knowledge recall.

---

## CLI Design (`main.py`)

Single entry point with three modes:

```bash
# REPL mode (default) — interactive question-answering loop
python main.py

# Single question mode
python main.py "Who invented the telephone?"

# Demo mode — runs curated example questions automatically
python main.py --demo

# Eval mode
python run_evals.py --prompt v1 --output results/v1_results.json
python run_evals.py --prompt v2 --output results/v2_results.json
python run_evals.py --compare results/v1_results.json results/v2_results.json
```

REPL shows: answer + which Wikipedia articles were searched + search count. `--demo` feeds 5-6 interesting example questions that showcase multi-hop, disambiguation, and misconception handling.

---

## Project Structure

```
anthropic_qa_takehome/
  main.py               # REPL + demo mode
  run_evals.py          # Eval runner CLI
  requirements.txt
  .env.example
  README.md

  src/
    agent.py            # Agentic loop: tool calling, multi-hop, response synthesis
    tools.py            # search_wikipedia() — MediaWiki API wrapper
    prompts.py          # PROMPT_V1 and PROMPT_V2 (versioned constants)

  evals/
    dataset/
      hotpotqa.py       # HotpotQA loader + subset selector
      curated.py        # Hand-crafted adversarial + edge cases
      schema.py         # TestCase dataclass
    checks.py           # All deterministic check functions
    judges/
      grounding.py      # Judge: is answer grounded in retrieved Wikipedia text?
      factuality.py     # Judge: is answer factually correct?
      query_quality.py  # Judge: were search queries well-formulated?
    runner.py           # Orchestrates deterministic + judges + pairwise

  results/              # Committed JSON output for reviewer inspection
    v1_results.json
    v2_results.json
    comparison.json
```

---

## Dataset Design

### Schema

```python
@dataclass
class TestCase:
    id: str
    question: str
    category: str           # See categories below
    expected_answer: str    # Short answer string (for deterministic matching)
    key_facts: list[str]    # Substrings that should appear in the answer
    supporting_articles: list[str]  # Wikipedia titles the agent should search
    requires_multihop: bool
    adversarial_type: str | None  # "misconception", "disambiguation", "refusal", "false_premise"
    notes: str              # Why this case is interesting / what it tests
```

### Part 1: HotpotQA Subset (~40 cases)

HotpotQA (`datasets` library, `hotpot_qa` on Hugging Face) is ideal because:
- Multi-hop questions explicitly require chaining two Wikipedia articles
- Each question has: `answer` (short string), `supporting_facts` (list of Wikipedia article titles)
- `supporting_facts` titles → perfect deterministic check for retrieval quality
- Dataset from 2018 — filter to time-stable facts (historical, scientific, biographical)

**Selection criteria:**
- Type: `bridge` (not comparison — bridge questions require sequential lookup)
- Level: `medium` and `hard` (easy ones are often solvable without multi-hop)
- Filter out time-sensitive questions (elections, records, "current" anything)
- Target: ~40 cases

**HotpotQA bridge question pattern:**
> "What is the nationality of the director of [Film]?"
> → requires: search Film → find director name → search director → find nationality

This is exactly what we want to test. The `supporting_facts` field gives us the two Wikipedia article titles to check against.

### Part 2: Curated Cases (~20 cases)

Hand-crafted to test specific failure modes and adversarial scenarios.

#### Simple Factual (5 cases)
Baseline — single Wikipedia lookup, clear ground truth.
- "When was Marie Curie born?" → "1867", supporting: ["Marie Curie"]
- "What is the capital of New Zealand?" → "Wellington", supporting: ["Wellington"]
- "Who wrote Crime and Punishment?" → "Fyodor Dostoevsky", supporting: ["Crime and Punishment"]

#### Misconceptions (4 cases)
Common false beliefs — tests whether agent faithfully reports what Wikipedia says vs. propagating myths.
- "Did Einstein fail math in school?" → Wikipedia explicitly debunks this
- "Was Napoleon unusually short?" → Wikipedia addresses the myth (he was ~5'7", average for his time)
- "Does the Great Wall of China visible from space?" → Wikipedia says no
- Check: `no_false_claim` — the false belief should NOT appear in the answer

#### Entity Disambiguation (3 cases)
Multiple possible Wikipedia articles — tests query refinement and context sensitivity.
- "Tell me about Mercury" → planet? element? Roman god? Agent should clarify or pick most plausible
- "Who is Michael Jordan?" → athlete vs. physicist (Michael I. Jordan)
- "What is Python?" → programming language vs. snake

#### False Premise Questions (3 cases)
Questions that embed an incorrect assumption.
- "How many senses do humans have?" → premise is "5 senses," Wikipedia says more
- "Who invented the lightbulb?" → attribution is nuanced (Edison didn't invent it, he improved it)
- Agent should correct the premise, not just answer the question

#### Refusal / Missing Coverage (3 cases)
Questions where Wikipedia genuinely doesn't have sufficient information.
- Very obscure local figure not on Wikipedia
- Extremely recent event post-Wikipedia update cycle
- Check: agent should say it couldn't find reliable information, not hallucinate

#### Compound / Synthesis (2 cases)
Single question requiring synthesis across multiple facts from one article.
- "What did Marie Curie win Nobel Prizes for, and in what years?" → both Physics and Chemistry + years

---

## Deterministic Checks Design (`evals/checks.py`)

All checks are pure functions: `check_fn(case: TestCase, response: AgentResponse) -> CheckResult`.

```python
@dataclass
class CheckResult:
    passed: bool
    name: str
    details: str
```

### Category 1: Structural / Behavioral

| Check | Logic |
|-------|-------|
| `search_invoked` | `len(response.searches) >= 1` |
| `multihop_search_used` | For multi-hop cases: `len(response.searches) >= 2` |
| `required_articles_searched` | For HotpotQA: fuzzy match between query strings and expected supporting article titles (check if agent searched for the right entities) |
| `answered_not_refused` | Answer doesn't contain "I cannot", "I don't have", "no information" for cases that should be answerable |
| `correctly_refused` | For refusal-expected cases: answer contains hedging/uncertainty language |

### Category 2: Answer Content

| Check | Logic |
|-------|-------|
| `exact_match` | `case.expected_answer.lower() == extract_short_answer(response.answer).lower()` |
| `substring_match` | `case.expected_answer.lower() in response.answer.lower()` |
| `all_key_facts_present` | All strings in `case.key_facts` are substrings of the answer |
| `any_key_facts_present` | At least one key fact present (softer check for complex answers) |
| `no_false_claim` | For misconception cases: `case.adversarial_claim not in response.answer.lower()` |
| `year_present` | For date questions: the expected year appears |

### Category 3: Quality Heuristics

| Check | Logic |
|-------|-------|
| `answer_length_reasonable` | 20 < len(answer) < 3000 chars |
| `cites_source` | Answer references the Wikipedia article title(s) searched |
| `no_url_hallucination` | Answer doesn't contain made-up Wikipedia URLs |

### HotpotQA-Specific: `required_articles_searched`

Key design: for each HotpotQA case, `supporting_articles` contains 2 Wikipedia titles.
Check uses fuzzy match: for each required title, does any search query have >60% token overlap?

```python
def required_articles_searched(case, response):
    for required_title in case.supporting_articles:
        title_tokens = set(required_title.lower().split())
        found = any(
            len(title_tokens & set(q.lower().split())) / len(title_tokens) > 0.6
            for q in [s.query for s in response.searches]
        )
        if not found:
            return CheckResult(False, ..., f"Never searched for: {required_title}")
    return CheckResult(True, ...)
```

---

## LLM Judges — Specialized Per Dimension

All judges: Claude Haiku 4.5, return `{score: int 1-5, reasoning: str}`.

### Grounding Judge
- **Input**: question + all retrieved Wikipedia text + answer
- **Asks**: "Does this answer rely only on what Wikipedia says? Does it add facts not in the retrieved text?"
- **1** = heavy hallucination beyond Wikipedia, **5** = fully grounded with no additions

### Factuality Judge
- **Input**: question + answer + ground truth answer
- **Asks**: "Is this answer factually correct? Does it contradict the expected answer?"
- **1** = factually wrong, **5** = correct and complete

### Query Quality Judge
- **Input**: original question + list of all search queries used
- **Asks**: "Were these Wikipedia search queries well-formulated? Did they use specific entity names rather than full questions? Would these queries retrieve the right articles?"
- **1** = poor (full questions, vague), **5** = precise entity-name queries

---

## Pairwise Comparison

For each test case, compare v1 and v2 head-to-head:
- **Input**: question + answer_v1 + answer_v2 + retrieved context
- **Asks**: "Which answer is better overall? Consider: factual accuracy, grounding, completeness, query efficiency."
- **Output**: `{winner: "v1"|"v2"|"tie", confidence: "clear"|"slight", reasoning: str}`
- Aggregate: pairwise win rate is the headline metric for the iteration story

---

## System Prompts

### PROMPT_V1 — Initial

Key elements:
1. Role: Wikipedia-grounded research assistant
2. Rule: ALWAYS search Wikipedia before answering
3. Query guidance: entity names, not full questions (with examples)
4. Multi-hop: search again if first result is insufficient
5. Grounding: only claim what Wikipedia explicitly states
6. Uncertainty: say so clearly when Wikipedia doesn't cover it
7. Citation: reference the Wikipedia article(s) you used

### PROMPT_V2 — Post-Eval Iteration

Derived from real v1 failures. Expected failure modes:
- Multi-hop not triggered (model stops after one search) → add explicit agentic loop instruction with worked example
- Query verbosity (model sends full question as query) → add anti-example + format requirement  
- Grounding leaks (model adds training knowledge) → add explicit prohibition with example
- Disambiguation failure (model picks wrong entity) → add disambiguation guidance

---

## Iteration Story (Narrative for Writeup)

1. Build PROMPT_V1 → run full eval → `v1_results.json`
2. Analyze: find top failure categories by deterministic check failures and low judge scores
3. Write PROMPT_V2 targeting the 2-3 most impactful issues
4. Run full eval → `v2_results.json`
5. Pairwise comparison → `comparison.json`
6. Report: per-dimension delta, pairwise win rate, failure mode analysis

---

## Implementation Order

1. `src/tools.py` — Wikipedia search (10 min)
2. `src/prompts.py` — PROMPT_V1 (20 min)  
3. `src/agent.py` — agentic loop (20 min)
4. `main.py` — REPL + demo mode (15 min)
5. `evals/dataset/schema.py` + `curated.py` (25 min)
6. `evals/dataset/hotpotqa.py` — HotpotQA loader + subset (20 min)
7. `evals/checks.py` — all deterministic checks (20 min)
8. `evals/judges/` — 3 specialized judges (25 min)
9. `evals/runner.py` — orchestration + pairwise (20 min)
10. Run v1 evals → analyze → write PROMPT_V2 (30 min)
11. Run v2 evals → generate comparison (10 min)
12. README + written rationale (20 min)

**Estimated total: ~3.5 hours**

---

## Model Choices

- **Agent**: `claude-sonnet-4-6` — strongest reasoning for multi-hop
- **Judges**: `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` — fast, cheap, good enough for evaluation
- Both noted in writeup with rationale

## Approved Plan:
# Wikipedia QA Agent — Design Document

## Context

Anthropic take-home for Prompt Engineer (Agent Prompts & Evals). Primary evaluation criteria: prompt engineering quality, eval suite rigor and design thinking, authentic iteration loop. Code quality is secondary.

**Core design stance:** Agent *always* retrieves from Wikipedia before answering. Mandatory grounding — this is a Wikipedia-grounded assistant, not knowledge recall.

---

## CLI Design (`main.py`)

Single entry point with three modes:

```bash
# REPL mode (default) — interactive question-answering loop
python main.py

# Single question mode
python main.py "Who invented the telephone?"

# Demo mode — runs curated example questions automatically
python main.py --demo

# Eval mode
python run_evals.py --prompt v1 --output results/v1_results.json
python run_evals.py --prompt v2 --output results/v2_results.json
python run_evals.py --compare results/v1_results.json results/v2_results.json
```

REPL shows: answer + which Wikipedia articles were searched + search count. `--demo` feeds 5-6 interesting example questions that showcase multi-hop, disambiguation, and misconception handling.

---

## Project Structure

```
anthropic_qa_takehome/
  main.py               # REPL + demo mode
  run_evals.py          # Eval runner CLI
  requirements.txt
  .env.example
  README.md

  src/
    agent.py            # Agentic loop: tool calling, multi-hop, response synthesis
    tools.py            # search_wikipedia() — MediaWiki API wrapper
    prompts.py          # PROMPT_V1 and PROMPT_V2 (versioned constants)

  evals/
    dataset/
      hotpotqa.py       # HotpotQA loader + subset selector
      curated.py        # Hand-crafted adversarial + edge cases
      schema.py         # TestCase dataclass
    checks.py           # All deterministic check functions
    judges/
      grounding.py      # Judge: is answer grounded in retrieved Wikipedia text?
      factuality.py     # Judge: is answer factually correct?
      query_quality.py  # Judge: were search queries well-formulated?
    runner.py           # Orchestrates deterministic + judges + pairwise

  results/              # Committed JSON output for reviewer inspection
    v1_results.json
    v2_results.json
    comparison.json
```

---

## Dataset Design

### Schema

```python
@dataclass
class TestCase:
    id: str
    question: str
    category: str           # See categories below
    expected_answer: str    # Short answer string (for deterministic matching)
    key_facts: list[str]    # Substrings that should appear in the answer
    supporting_articles: list[str]  # Wikipedia titles the agent should search
    requires_multihop: bool
    adversarial_type: str | None  # "misconception", "disambiguation", "refusal", "false_premise"
    notes: str              # Why this case is interesting / what it tests
```

### Part 1: HotpotQA Subset (~40 cases)

HotpotQA (`datasets` library, `hotpot_qa` on Hugging Face) is ideal because:
- Multi-hop questions explicitly require chaining two Wikipedia articles
- Each question has: `answer` (short string), `supporting_facts` (list of Wikipedia article titles)
- `supporting_facts` titles → perfect deterministic check for retrieval quality
- Dataset from 2018 — filter to time-stable facts (historical, scientific, biographical)

**Selection criteria:**
- Type: `bridge` (not comparison — bridge questions require sequential lookup)
- Level: `medium` and `hard` (easy ones are often solvable without multi-hop)
- Filter out time-sensitive questions (elections, records, "current" anything)
- Target: ~40 cases

**HotpotQA bridge question pattern:**
> "What is the nationality of the director of [Film]?"
> → requires: search Film → find director name → search director → find nationality

This is exactly what we want to test. The `supporting_facts` field gives us the two Wikipedia article titles to check against.

### Part 2: Curated Cases (~20 cases)

Hand-crafted to test specific failure modes and adversarial scenarios.

#### Simple Factual (5 cases)
Baseline — single Wikipedia lookup, clear ground truth.
- "When was Marie Curie born?" → "1867", supporting: ["Marie Curie"]
- "What is the capital of New Zealand?" → "Wellington", supporting: ["Wellington"]
- "Who wrote Crime and Punishment?" → "Fyodor Dostoevsky", supporting: ["Crime and Punishment"]

#### Misconceptions (4 cases)
Common false beliefs — tests whether agent faithfully reports what Wikipedia says vs. propagating myths.
- "Did Einstein fail math in school?" → Wikipedia explicitly debunks this
- "Was Napoleon unusually short?" → Wikipedia addresses the myth (he was ~5'7", average for his time)
- "Does the Great Wall of China visible from space?" → Wikipedia says no
- Check: `no_false_claim` — the false belief should NOT appear in the answer

#### Entity Disambiguation (3 cases)
Multiple possible Wikipedia articles — tests query refinement and context sensitivity.
- "Tell me about Mercury" → planet? element? Roman god? Agent should clarify or pick most plausible
- "Who is Michael Jordan?" → athlete vs. physicist (Michael I. Jordan)
- "What is Python?" → programming language vs. snake

#### False Premise Questions (3 cases)
Questions that embed an incorrect assumption.
- "How many senses do humans have?" → premise is "5 senses," Wikipedia says more
- "Who invented the lightbulb?" → attribution is nuanced (Edison didn't invent it, he improved it)
- Agent should correct the premise, not just answer the question

#### Refusal / Missing Coverage (3 cases)
Questions where Wikipedia genuinely doesn't have sufficient information.
- Very obscure local figure not on Wikipedia
- Extremely recent event post-Wikipedia update cycle
- Check: agent should say it couldn't find reliable information, not hallucinate

#### Compound / Synthesis (2 cases)
Single question requiring synthesis across multiple facts from one article.
- "What did Marie Curie win Nobel Prizes for, and in what years?" → both Physics and Chemistry + years

---

## Deterministic Checks Design (`evals/checks.py`)

All checks are pure functions: `check_fn(case: TestCase, response: AgentResponse) -> CheckResult`.

```python
@dataclass
class CheckResult:
    passed: bool
    name: str
    details: str
```

### Category 1: Structural / Behavioral

| Check | Logic |
|-------|-------|
| `search_invoked` | `len(response.searches) >= 1` |
| `multihop_search_used` | For multi-hop cases: `len(response.searches) >= 2` |
| `required_articles_searched` | For HotpotQA: fuzzy match between query strings and expected supporting article titles (check if agent searched for the right entities) |
| `answered_not_refused` | Answer doesn't contain "I cannot", "I don't have", "no information" for cases that should be answerable |
| `correctly_refused` | For refusal-expected cases: answer contains hedging/uncertainty language |

### Category 2: Answer Content

| Check | Logic |
|-------|-------|
| `exact_match` | `case.expected_answer.lower() == extract_short_answer(response.answer).lower()` |
| `substring_match` | `case.expected_answer.lower() in response.answer.lower()` |
| `all_key_facts_present` | All strings in `case.key_facts` are substrings of the answer |
| `any_key_facts_present` | At least one key fact present (softer check for complex answers) |
| `no_false_claim` | For misconception cases: `case.adversarial_claim not in response.answer.lower()` |
| `year_present` | For date questions: the expected year appears |

### Category 3: Quality Heuristics

| Check | Logic |
|-------|-------|
| `answer_length_reasonable` | 20 < len(answer) < 3000 chars |
| `cites_source` | Answer references the Wikipedia article title(s) searched |
| `no_url_hallucination` | Answer doesn't contain made-up Wikipedia URLs |

### HotpotQA-Specific: `required_articles_searched`

Key design: for each HotpotQA case, `supporting_articles` contains 2 Wikipedia titles.
Check uses fuzzy match: for each required title, does any search query have >60% token overlap?

```python
def required_articles_searched(case, response):
    for required_title in case.supporting_articles:
        title_tokens = set(required_title.lower().split())
        found = any(
            len(title_tokens & set(q.lower().split())) / len(title_tokens) > 0.6
            for q in [s.query for s in response.searches]
        )
        if not found:
            return CheckResult(False, ..., f"Never searched for: {required_title}")
    return CheckResult(True, ...)
```

---

## LLM Judges — Specialized Per Dimension

All judges: Claude Haiku 4.5, return `{score: int 1-5, reasoning: str}`.

### Grounding Judge
- **Input**: question + all retrieved Wikipedia text + answer
- **Asks**: "Does this answer rely only on what Wikipedia says? Does it add facts not in the retrieved text?"
- **1** = heavy hallucination beyond Wikipedia, **5** = fully grounded with no additions

### Factuality Judge
- **Input**: question + answer + ground truth answer
- **Asks**: "Is this answer factually correct? Does it contradict the expected answer?"
- **1** = factually wrong, **5** = correct and complete

### Query Quality Judge
- **Input**: original question + list of all search queries used
- **Asks**: "Were these Wikipedia search queries well-formulated? Did they use specific entity names rather than full questions? Would these queries retrieve the right articles?"
- **1** = poor (full questions, vague), **5** = precise entity-name queries

---

## Pairwise Comparison

For each test case, compare v1 and v2 head-to-head:
- **Input**: question + answer_v1 + answer_v2 + retrieved context
- **Asks**: "Which answer is better overall? Consider: factual accuracy, grounding, completeness, query efficiency."
- **Output**: `{winner: "v1"|"v2"|"tie", confidence: "clear"|"slight", reasoning: str}`
- Aggregate: pairwise win rate is the headline metric for the iteration story

---

## System Prompts

### PROMPT_V1 — Initial

Key elements:
1. Role: Wikipedia-grounded research assistant
2. Rule: ALWAYS search Wikipedia before answering
3. Query guidance: entity names, not full questions (with examples)
4. Multi-hop: search again if first result is insufficient
5. Grounding: only claim what Wikipedia explicitly states
6. Uncertainty: say so clearly when Wikipedia doesn't cover it
7. Citation: reference the Wikipedia article(s) you used

### PROMPT_V2 — Post-Eval Iteration

Derived from real v1 failures. Expected failure modes:
- Multi-hop not triggered (model stops after one search) → add explicit agentic loop instruction with worked example
- Query verbosity (model sends full question as query) → add anti-example + format requirement  
- Grounding leaks (model adds training knowledge) → add explicit prohibition with example
- Disambiguation failure (model picks wrong entity) → add disambiguation guidance

---

## Iteration Story (Narrative for Writeup)

1. Build PROMPT_V1 → run full eval → `v1_results.json`
2. Analyze: find top failure categories by deterministic check failures and low judge scores
3. Write PROMPT_V2 targeting the 2-3 most impactful issues
4. Run full eval → `v2_results.json`
5. Pairwise comparison → `comparison.json`
6. Report: per-dimension delta, pairwise win rate, failure mode analysis

---

## Implementation Order

1. `src/tools.py` — Wikipedia search (10 min)
2. `src/prompts.py` — PROMPT_V1 (20 min)  
3. `src/agent.py` — agentic loop (20 min)
4. `main.py` — REPL + demo mode (15 min)
5. `evals/dataset/schema.py` + `curated.py` (25 min)
6. `evals/dataset/hotpotqa.py` — HotpotQA loader + subset (20 min)
7. `evals/checks.py` — all deterministic checks (20 min)
8. `evals/judges/` — 3 specialized judges (25 min)
9. `evals/runner.py` — orchestration + pairwise (20 min)
10. Run v1 evals → analyze → write PROMPT_V2 (30 min)
11. Run v2 evals → generate comparison (10 min)
12. README + written rationale (20 min)

**Estimated total: ~3.5 hours**

/Users/dilraj/Documents/Development/anthropic_qa_takehome/src/tools.py
"""Wikipedia search via the MediaWiki API."""

import re
import urllib.parse
import requests

MEDIAWIKI_API = "https://en.wikipedia.org/w/api.php"
EXTRACT_WORD_LIMIT = 600
_session = requests.Session()
_session.headers["User-Agent"] = "WikipediaQA-Agent/1.0 (research project)"


def search_wikipedia(query: str) -> dict:
    """Search Wikipedia and return the best-matching article's content.

    Returns a dict with keys: title, content, url, error (if nothing found).
    """
    title = _find_best_title(query)
    if title is None:
        return {"error": f"No Wikipedia article found for: {query!r}"}
    content = _fetch_extract(title)
    if content is None:
        return {"error": f"Could not retrieve content for Wikipedia article: {title!r}"}
    return {
        "title": title,
        "content": _truncate_to_words(content, EXTRACT_WORD_LIMIT),
        "url": f"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/{urllib.parse.quote(title.replace(' ', '_'))}",
    }


def _find_best_title(query: str) -> str | None:
    """Return the best Wikipedia article title for a query.

    Tries exact/redirect resolution first, falls back to full-text search.
    """
    # Try direct page lookup (handles redirects automatically)
    resp = _session.get(MEDIAWIKI_API, params={
        "action": "query",
        "titles": query,
        "redirects": 1,
        "format": "json",
    }, timeout=10)
    resp.raise_for_status()
    pages = resp.json()["query"]["pages"]
    page = next(iter(pages.values()))
    if "missing" not in page:
        return page["title"]

    # Fall back to search
    resp = _session.get(MEDIAWIKI_API, params={
        "action": "query",
        "list": "search",
        "srsearch": query,
        "srlimit": 1,
        "srinfo": "",
        "srprop": "titlesnippet",
        "format": "json",
    }, timeout=10)
    resp.raise_for_status()
    results = resp.json()["query"]["search"]
    if not results:
        return None
    return results[0]["title"]


def _fetch_extract(title: str) -> str | None:
    """Fetch the plain-text intro extract for a Wikipedia article."""
    resp = _session.get(MEDIAWIKI_API, params={
        "action": "query",
        "prop": "extracts",
        "exintro": 1,       # intro section only
        "explaintext": 1,   # plain text, no markup
        "titles": title,
        "redirects": 1,
        "format": "json",
    }, timeout=10)
    resp.raise_for_status()
    pages = resp.json()["query"]["pages"]
    page = next(iter(pages.values()))
    return page.get("extract") or None


def _truncate_to_words(text: str, limit: int) -> str:
    words = re.split(r"\s+", text.strip())
    if len(words) <= limit:
        return text.strip()
    return " ".join(words[:limit]) + " [...]"

"""System prompts for the Wikipedia QA agent. Versioned for eval comparison."""

PROMPT_V1 = """\
You are a Wikipedia-grounded research assistant. Your job is to answer questions \
accurately by always consulting Wikipedia first — you do not rely on your training \
knowledge to make factual claims.

## Core rules

1. **Always search before answering.** For any factual question, call \
search_wikipedia before writing your answer. Never answer from memory alone.

2. **Only state what Wikipedia confirms.** If the retrieved article doesn't \
mention something, don't say it. Clearly distinguish between what Wikipedia \
says and what you are uncertain about.

3. **Search again if needed.** If the first article doesn't fully answer the \
question — for example, you found that Person A directed Film B, but you still \
need to know Person A's nationality — call search_wikipedia again for the \
missing piece. You may search as many times as needed.

4. **Cite your sources.** In your final answer, name the Wikipedia article(s) \
you consulted (e.g., "According to the Wikipedia article on Marie Curie...").

5. **Say when you don't know.** If Wikipedia doesn't have enough information \
to answer the question, say so clearly. Don't guess or fabricate.

## Query formulation

Wikipedia search works best with specific entity names, not full questions.

- Bad query: "What year did Albert Einstein die?"
- Good query: "Albert Einstein"

- Bad query: "Who invented the telephone and where were they born?"
- Good query: "Alexander Graham Bell"

Use the most specific, well-known name for the entity you're looking for.

## Multi-step reasoning

For questions that require connecting multiple facts:
1. Identify what you need to look up first.
2. Search for it.
3. Extract the intermediate fact you need.
4. Search for that next entity.
5. Synthesize all retrieved information into your answer.

Example: "What is the birth country of the composer of Bohemian Rhapsody?"
→ Search "Bohemian Rhapsody" → discover it was written by Freddie Mercury
→ Search "Freddie Mercury" → find he was born in Zanzibar (now Tanzania)
→ Answer with both sources cited.
"""

PROMPT_V2 = PROMPT_V1  # Placeholder — will be updated after v1 eval results

📝 Write agent.py
/Users/dilraj/Documents/Development/anthropic_qa_takehome/src/agent.py
"""Wikipedia QA agent: agentic loop with multi-hop tool use."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import anthropic
from dotenv import load_dotenv

from src.tools import search_wikipedia
from src.prompts import PROMPT_V1, PROMPT_V2

load_dotenv()

AGENT_MODEL = "claude-sonnet-4-6"
MAX_ITERATIONS = 8

TOOL_DEFINITION = {
    "name": "search_wikipedia",
    "description": (
        "Search Wikipedia for a topic and retrieve the introductory section of the "
        "best-matching article. Returns the article title, plain-text content "
        "(up to ~600 words), and the Wikipedia URL. Use specific entity names as "
        "queries (e.g., 'Marie Curie', 'French Revolution') rather than full questions."
    ),
    "input_schema": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
            "query": {
                "type": "string",
                "description": "A specific entity name or topic to search for on Wikipedia.",
            }
        },
        "required": ["query"],
    },
}

PROMPT_MAP = {"v1": PROMPT_V1, "v2": PROMPT_V2}


@dataclass
class SearchResult:
    query: str
    title: str | None
    content: str | None
    url: str | None
    error: str | None = None


@dataclass
class AgentResponse:
    answer: str
    searches: list[SearchResult] = field(default_factory=list)
    prompt_version: str = "v1"
    model: str = AGENT_MODEL
    iterations: int = 0


def ask(question: str, prompt_version: str = "v1") -> AgentResponse:
    """Run the Wikipedia QA agent on a single question."""
    client = anthropic.Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])
    system_prompt = PROMPT_MAP.get(prompt_version, PROMPT_V1)

    messages: list[dict] = [{"role": "user", "content": question}]
    searches: list[SearchResult] = []
    iterations = 0

    while iterations < MAX_ITERATIONS:
        iterations += 1
        response = client.messages.create(
            model=AGENT_MODEL,
            max_tokens=1024,
            system=system_prompt,
            tools=[TOOL_DEFINITION],
            messages=messages,
        )

        # Append assistant turn
        messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": response.content})

        if response.stop_reason == "end_turn":
            # Extract text answer from final response
            answer = _extract_text(response.content)
            return AgentResponse(
                answer=answer,
                searches=searches,
                prompt_version=prompt_version,
                model=AGENT_MODEL,
                iterations=iterations,
            )

        if response.stop_reason == "tool_use":
            tool_results = []
            for block in response.content:
                if block.type != "tool_use":
                    continue
                query = block.input.get("query", "")
                result = search_wikipedia(query)
                sr = SearchResult(
                    query=query,
                    title=result.get("title"),
                    content=result.get("content"),
                    url=result.get("url"),
                    error=result.get("error"),
                )
                searches.append(sr)
                tool_results.append({
                    "type": "tool_result",
                    "tool_use_id": block.id,
                    "content": _format_tool_result(result),
                })
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": tool_results})
        else:
            # Unexpected stop reason — extract whatever text we have
            answer = _extract_text(response.content)
            return AgentResponse(
                answer=answer or "(no answer generated)",
                searches=searches,
                prompt_version=prompt_version,
                model=AGENT_MODEL,
                iterations=iterations,
            )

    # Exceeded max iterations — force a final answer
    response = client.messages.create(
        model=AGENT_MODEL,
        max_tokens=1024,
        system=system_prompt,
        tools=[TOOL_DEFINITION],
        tool_choice={"type": "none"},
        messages=messages + [{
            "role": "user",
            "content": "Please provide your final answer based on what you've found so far.",
        }],
    )
    return AgentResponse(
        answer=_extract_text(response.content) or "(max iterations reached)",
        searches=searches,
        prompt_version=prompt_version,
        model=AGENT_MODEL,
        iterations=iterations,
    )


def _extract_text(content: list) -> str:
    return " ".join(
        block.text for block in content if hasattr(block, "text")
    ).strip()


def _format_tool_result(result: dict) -> str:
    if "error" in result:
        return f"[Wikipedia search error: {result['error']}]"
    return (
        f"Title: {result['title']}\n"
        f"URL: {result['url']}\n\n"
        f"{result['content']}"
    )

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Wikipedia QA Agent — interactive REPL, demo mode, and single-question mode."""

import argparse
import sys

from rich.console import Console
from rich.markdown import Markdown
from rich.panel import Panel
from rich.rule import Rule
from rich.text import Text

from src.agent import ask

console = Console()

DEMO_QUESTIONS = [
    "Who was the first person to walk on the moon, and what was the name of the mission?",
    "What is the nationality of the director of the film Parasite?",
    "Did Einstein fail math in school?",
    "Who invented the telephone?",
    "What did Marie Curie win Nobel Prizes for, and in what years?",
    "Is the Great Wall of China visible from space?",
]


def run_question(question: str, prompt_version: str = "v1") -> None:
    console.print(f"\n[bold cyan]Question:[/bold cyan] {question}\n")
    with console.status("[dim]Searching Wikipedia...[/dim]", spinner="dots"):
        result = ask(question, prompt_version=prompt_version)

    # Show search trace
    if result.searches:
        console.print(Rule("[dim]Wikipedia searches[/dim]", style="dim"))
        for i, s in enumerate(result.searches, 1):
            if s.error:
                console.print(f"  [dim]{i}.[/dim] [red]✗[/red] [dim]{s.query!r}[/dim] → {s.error}")
            else:
                console.print(
                    f"  [dim]{i}.[/dim] [green]✓[/green] [dim]{s.query!r}[/dim] "
                    f"→ [link={s.url}]{s.title}[/link]"
                )
        console.print()

    # Show answer
    console.print(Rule("[dim]Answer[/dim]", style="dim"))
    console.print(Markdown(result.answer))
    console.print(
        f"\n[dim]  ({result.iterations} iteration(s), "
        f"prompt={result.prompt_version}, model={result.model})[/dim]\n"
    )


def repl_mode(prompt_version: str) -> None:
    console.print(Panel(
        Text.from_markup(
            "[bold]Wikipedia QA Agent[/bold]\n\n"
            "Ask any factual question — the agent always retrieves evidence from Wikipedia.\n"
            "Type [bold cyan]exit[/bold cyan] or press [bold cyan]Ctrl-C[/bold cyan] to quit.",
        ),
        title="[bold green]Ready[/bold green]",
        border_style="green",
    ))
    while True:
        try:
            question = console.input("\n[bold cyan]>[/bold cyan] ").strip()
        except (KeyboardInterrupt, EOFError):
            console.print("\n[dim]Goodbye.[/dim]")
            break
        if not question:
            continue
        if question.lower() in {"exit", "quit", "q"}:
            console.print("[dim]Goodbye.[/dim]")
            break
        run_question(question, prompt_version=prompt_version)


def demo_mode(prompt_version: str) -> None:
    console.print(Panel(
        "[bold]Wikipedia QA Agent — Demo Mode[/bold]\n\n"
        "Running a curated set of questions that showcase multi-hop reasoning,\n"
        "misconception handling, and disambiguation.",
        title="[bold yellow]Demo[/bold yellow]",
        border_style="yellow",
    ))
    for q in DEMO_QUESTIONS:
        run_question(q, prompt_version=prompt_version)
        console.input("[dim]  Press Enter for next question...[/dim]")


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Wikipedia QA Agent",
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
        epilog=(
            "examples:\n"
            "  python main.py                          # interactive REPL\n"
            '  python main.py "Who invented the radio?" # single question\n'
            "  python main.py --demo                   # demo mode\n"
            "  python main.py --prompt v2              # use prompt version 2\n"
        ),
    )
    parser.add_argument("question", nargs="?", help="Ask a single question and exit")
    parser.add_argument("--demo", action="store_true", help="Run demo questions")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--prompt", choices=["v1", "v2"], default="v1",
        help="Prompt version to use (default: v1)",
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()

    if args.demo:
        demo_mode(args.prompt)
    elif args.question:
        run_question(args.question, prompt_version=args.prompt)
    else:
        repl_mode(args.prompt)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

"""TestCase schema for the Wikipedia QA eval suite."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field


@dataclass
class TestCase:
    id: str
    question: str
    category: str
    """One of: simple_factual, misconception, disambiguation, false_premise,
    refusal, compound, hotpotqa_bridge"""

    expected_answer: str
    """Short answer string used for deterministic substring matching."""

    key_facts: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    """Substrings that should appear in the answer (case-insensitive)."""

    supporting_articles: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    """Wikipedia article titles the agent is expected to search."""

    requires_multihop: bool = False
    """True if the question requires chaining two or more Wikipedia lookups."""

    adversarial_type: str | None = None
    """One of: misconception, disambiguation, false_premise, refusal — or None."""

    adversarial_claim: str | None = None
    """For misconception cases: the false claim that must NOT appear in the answer."""

    notes: str = ""
    """Explanation of what this case tests and why it's interesting."""

    source: str = "curated"
    """'curated' or 'hotpotqa'"""

Assistant2026-06-02T17:32:17.474Z

Good instinct to pause — let's get alignment on this before building more.

On tool definition versioning: You're exactly right. The tool description and the query parameter description are just as much prompt engineering as the system prompt. They tell the model when to call the tool and how to formulate queries — both things we'll iterate on. They need to be versioned together with the system prompt as a single atomic unit.

On text files vs. Python file: Here's the real tradeoff:

The current approach (constants in prompts.py) is fine for 2 versions, but it has a real problem: prompts are content, not code. Editing long strings in a .py file is awkward, you lose proper text/markdown highlighting, and Git diffs show Python syntax noise rather than clean text diffs. Reviewing a prompt change in a PR is much cleaner as a .md diff.

My recommendation: a directory per version, where each version bundles both the system prompt and tool definition together:

prompts/
  v1/
    system.md       ← system prompt text
    tool.md         ← just the description text for the search tool
  v2/
    system.md
    tool.md

A thin loader in src/prompts.py reads them at runtime. The agent builds the full tool definition by injecting whatever tool.md says into the schema. Adding a new version is as simple as mkdir prompts/v3 && cp prompts/v2/* prompts/v3/ and editing the text files — no Python touching required.

This also makes the eval's --prompt v1 flag unambiguous: it loads one directory, gets a complete (system prompt + tool definition) snapshot.

Two questions before I restructure this:

📝 Write system.md
/Users/dilraj/Documents/Development/anthropic_qa_takehome/prompts/v1/system.md
You are a Wikipedia-grounded research assistant. Your job is to answer questions accurately by always consulting Wikipedia first — you do not rely on your training knowledge to make factual claims.

## Core rules

1. **Always search before answering.** For any factual question, call search_wikipedia before writing your answer. Never answer from memory alone.

2. **Only state what Wikipedia confirms.** If the retrieved article doesn't mention something, don't say it. Clearly distinguish between what Wikipedia says and what you are uncertain about.

3. **Search again if needed.** If the first article doesn't fully answer the question — for example, you found that Person A directed Film B, but you still need to know Person A's nationality — call search_wikipedia again for the missing piece. You may search as many times as needed.

4. **Cite your sources.** In your final answer, name the Wikipedia article(s) you consulted (e.g., "According to the Wikipedia article on Marie Curie...").

5. **Say when you don't know.** If Wikipedia doesn't have enough information to answer the question, say so clearly. Don't guess or fabricate.

## Query formulation

Wikipedia search works best with specific entity names, not full questions.

- Bad query: "What year did Albert Einstein die?"
- Good query: "Albert Einstein"

- Bad query: "Who invented the telephone and where were they born?"
- Good query: "Alexander Graham Bell"

Use the most specific, well-known name for the entity you're looking for.

## Multi-step reasoning

For questions that require connecting multiple facts:
1. Identify what you need to look up first.
2. Search for it.
3. Extract the intermediate fact you need.
4. Search for that next entity.
5. Synthesize all retrieved information into your answer.

Example: "What is the birth country of the composer of Bohemian Rhapsody?"
→ Search "Bohemian Rhapsody" → discover it was written by Freddie Mercury
→ Search "Freddie Mercury" → find he was born in Zanzibar (now Tanzania)
→ Answer with both sources cited.

📝 Write prompts.py
/Users/dilraj/Documents/Development/anthropic_qa_takehome/src/prompts.py
"""Load versioned prompt files from the prompts/ directory."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PromptVersion:
    version: str
    system: str
    tool_description: str
    tool_query_description: str


def load(version: str) -> PromptVersion:
    """Load a prompt version from prompts/<version>/system.md and tool.md."""
    base = PROMPTS_DIR / version
    if not base.is_dir():
        available = [d.name for d in PROMPTS_DIR.iterdir() if d.is_dir()]
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown prompt version {version!r}. Available: {available}")

    system = (base / "system.md").read_text().strip()

    raw_tool = (base / "tool.md").read_text()
    parts = raw_tool.split("---", maxsplit=1)
    if len(parts) != 2:
        raise ValueError(f"prompts/{version}/tool.md must contain exactly one '---' separator")
    tool_description = parts[0].strip()
    tool_query_description = parts[1].strip()

    return PromptVersion(
        version=version,
        system=system,
        tool_description=tool_description,
        tool_query_description=tool_query_description,
    )


def available_versions() -> list[str]:
    return sorted(d.name for d in PROMPTS_DIR.iterdir() if d.is_dir())

48	    error: str | None = None
49	
50	
51	@dataclass
52	class AgentResponse:
53	    answer: str
54	    searches: list[SearchResult] = field(default_factory=list)
55	    prompt_version: str = "v1"
56	    model: str = AGENT_MODEL
57	    iterations: int = 0
58	
59	
60	def ask(question: str, prompt_version: str = "v1") -> AgentResponse:
61	    """Run the Wikipedia QA agent on a single question."""
62	    client = anthropic.Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])
63	    system_prompt = PROMPT_MAP.get(prompt_version, PROMPT_V1)
64	
65	    messages: list[dict] = [{"role": "user", "content": question}]
66	    searches: list[SearchResult] = []
67	    iterations = 0
68	
69	    while iterations < MAX_ITERATIONS:
70	        iterations += 1
71	        response = client.messages.create(
72	            model=AGENT_MODEL,
73	            max_tokens=1024,
74	            system=system_prompt,
75	            tools=[TOOL_DEFINITION],
76	            messages=messages,
77	        )
78	
79	        # Append assistant turn
80	        messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": response.content})
81	
82	        if response.stop_reason == "end_turn":
83	            # Extract text answer from final response
84	            answer = _extract_text(response.content)
85	            return AgentResponse(
86	                answer=answer,
87	                searches=searches,
88	                prompt_version=prompt_version,
89	                model=AGENT_MODEL,
90	                iterations=iterations,
91	            )
92	
93	        if response.stop_reason == "tool_use":
94	            tool_results = []
95	            for block in response.content:
96	                if block.type != "tool_use":
97	                    continue
98	                query = block.input.get("query", "")
99	                result = search_wikipedia(query)
100	                sr = SearchResult(
101	                    query=query,
102	                    title=result.get("title"),
103	                    content=result.get("content"),
104	                    url=result.get("url"),
105	                    error=result.get("error"),
106	                )
107	                searches.append(sr)
108	                tool_results.append({
109	                    "type": "tool_result",
110	                    "tool_use_id": block.id,
111	                    "content": _format_tool_result(result),
112	                })
113	            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": tool_results})
114	        else:
115	            # Unexpected stop reason — extract whatever text we have
116	            answer = _extract_text(response.content)
117	            return AgentResponse(
118	                answer=answer or "(no answer generated)",
119	                searches=searches,
120	                prompt_version=prompt_version,
121	                model=AGENT_MODEL,
122	                iterations=iterations,
123	            )
124	
125	    # Exceeded max iterations — force a final answer
126	    response = client.messages.create(
127	        model=AGENT_MODEL,
128	        max_tokens=1024,
129	        system=system_prompt,
130	        tools=[TOOL_DEFINITION],
131	        tool_choice={"type": "none"},
132	        messages=messages + [{
133	            "role": "user",
134	            "content": "Please provide your final answer based on what you've found so far.",
135	        }],
136	    )
137	    return AgentResponse(
138	        answer=_extract_text(response.content) or "(max iterations reached)",
139	        searches=searches,
140	        prompt_version=prompt_version,
141	        model=AGENT_MODEL,
142	        iterations=iterations,
143	    )
144	
145	
146	def _extract_text(content: list) -> str:
147	    return " ".join(
148	        block.text for block in content if hasattr(block, "text")
149	    ).strip()
150	
151	
152	def _format_tool_result(result: dict) -> str:
153	    if "error" in result:
154	        return f"[Wikipedia search error: {result['error']}]"
155	    return (
156	        f"Title: {result['title']}\n"
157	        f"URL: {result['url']}\n\n"
158	        f"{result['content']}"
159	    )
160

"""Wikipedia search via the MediaWiki API."""

import re
import time
import urllib.parse

import requests

MEDIAWIKI_API = "https://en.wikipedia.org/w/api.php"
EXTRACT_WORD_LIMIT = 600

_MAX_RETRIES = 3
_RETRY_BACKOFF = [1.0, 2.0, 4.0]  # seconds to wait before each retry

_session = requests.Session()
# Wikipedia requires a descriptive User-Agent; without one they rate-limit aggressively
_session.headers["User-Agent"] = (
    "WikipediaQA-ResearchAgent/1.0 "
    "(Anthropic take-home project; https://github.com/dilrajsinghdevgun)"
)


def search_wikipedia(query: str) -> dict:
    """Search Wikipedia and return the best-matching article's content.

    Returns a dict with keys: title, content, url
    — or {"error": "<message>"} if nothing was found or all retries failed.
    """
    try:
        title = _find_best_title(query)
    except Exception as exc:
        return {"error": f"Wikipedia search failed for {query!r}: {exc}"}

    if title is None:
        return {"error": f"No Wikipedia article found for: {query!r}"}

    try:
        content = _fetch_extract(title)
    except Exception as exc:
        return {"error": f"Could not retrieve Wikipedia article {title!r}: {exc}"}

    if not content:
        return {"error": f"Wikipedia article {title!r} has no extractable content"}

    return {
        "title": title,
        "content": _truncate_to_words(content, EXTRACT_WORD_LIMIT),
        "url": f"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/{urllib.parse.quote(title.replace(' ', '_'))}",
    }


def _get(params: dict) -> requests.Response:
    """GET with retry logic and exponential backoff on 429 / 5xx."""
    for attempt, wait in enumerate(_RETRY_BACKOFF):
        resp = _session.get(MEDIAWIKI_API, params=params, timeout=15)
        if resp.status_code == 429 or resp.status_code >= 500:
            if attempt < _MAX_RETRIES - 1:
                time.sleep(wait)
                continue
        resp.raise_for_status()
        return resp
    resp.raise_for_status()  # final attempt exhausted
    return resp  # unreachable but satisfies type checker


def _find_best_title(query: str) -> str | None:
    """Return the best Wikipedia article title for a query.

    Tries direct page resolution (handles redirects) first, falls back to search.
    """
    resp = _get({
        "action": "query",
        "titles": query,
        "redirects": 1,
        "format": "json",
    })
    pages = resp.json()["query"]["pages"]
    page = next(iter(pages.values()))
    if "missing" not in page:
        return page["title"]

    resp = _get({
        "action": "query",
        "list": "search",
        "srsearch": query,
        "srlimit": 1,
        "srinfo": "",
        "srprop": "titlesnippet",
        "format": "json",
    })
    results = resp.json()["query"]["search"]
    return results[0]["title"] if results else None


def _fetch_extract(title: str) -> str | None:
    """Fetch the plain-text intro extract for a Wikipedia article."""
    resp = _get({
        "action": "query",
        "prop": "extracts",
        "exintro": 1,
        "explaintext": 1,
        "titles": title,
        "redirects": 1,
        "format": "json",
    })
    pages = resp.json()["query"]["pages"]
    page = next(iter(pages.values()))
    return page.get("extract") or None


def _truncate_to_words(text: str, limit: int) -> str:
    words = re.split(r"\s+", text.strip())
    if len(words) <= limit:
        return text.strip()
    return " ".join(words[:limit]) + " [...]"

You are a Wikipedia-grounded research assistant. Your job is to answer questions accurately by always consulting Wikipedia first — you do not rely on your training knowledge to make factual claims.

## Core rules

1. **Always search before answering.** For any factual question, call search_wikipedia before writing your answer. Never answer from memory alone.

2. **Only state what Wikipedia confirms.** If the retrieved article doesn't mention something, don't say it. Clearly distinguish between what Wikipedia says and what you are uncertain about.

3. **Search again if needed.** If the first article doesn't fully answer the question — for example, you found that Person A directed Film B, but you still need to know Person A's nationality — call search_wikipedia again for the missing piece. You may search as many times as needed.

4. **Cite your sources.** In your final answer, name the Wikipedia article(s) you consulted (e.g., "According to the Wikipedia article on Marie Curie...").

5. **Say when you don't know.** If Wikipedia doesn't have enough information to answer the question, say so clearly. Don't guess or fabricate.

## Query formulation

Wikipedia search works best with specific entity names, not full questions.

- Bad query: "What year did Albert Einstein die?"
- Good query: "Albert Einstein"

- Bad query: "Who invented the telephone and where were they born?"
- Good query: "Alexander Graham Bell"

Use the most specific, well-known name for the entity you're looking for.

## Sources

At the end produce your sources

Assistant2026-06-02T17:40:18.334Z

Got it — v1 is intentionally sparse. The removed parts create predictable, measurable failure modes: - No multi-step reasoning guidance → the model will struggle to chain searches (just "search again if needed" with no structure) - Minimal sourcing instruction → "produce your sources" is vague, citation format will be inconsistent

That's a clean eval story: run v1, observe those specific failures, write v2 that addresses them.

One tool design issue worth discussing separately from the prompt: I found that the Neymar injury info is in the article body, not the intro. Our exintro=1 flag returns only the intro section — that's why 8 searches still came up empty. This isn't a prompt failure, it's a retrieval ceiling: no matter how good the query is, the model can't find facts that aren't in the content we return.

Two options: - Keep intro-only (current): Faster, less context. Model must search the right event article ("Brazil v Colombia 2014 FIFA World Cup quarter-final") rather than a person article. This is actually a harder, more interesting prompting challenge — the model needs to know that when a person article doesn't have the answer, it should search for the specific event. - Drop exintro flag, return 800–1000 words: Gets deeper into articles. Many "specific career fact" questions start working. Less interesting prompting challenge, but more realistic tool behavior.

My lean: drop exintro, increase to ~800 words. The intro-only limit is an artificial constraint that makes the tool unreliable in a way that doesn't help the eval story — we want failures driven by prompt quality, not tool design. But this is your call since it affects the kind of prompt engineering challenges you're showcasing.

What would you like to do here, and what else did you want to test before we move to the dataset?
